{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Kv9gq4VExJUASWvLaKtiZC","type":"tool_result","content":"# Deliver a user's conversation list over the WebSocket protocol\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#144\nLabels: type:feature, size:M, area:api, area:compliance, epic:extension-points\n\nPart of #35\n\n## Why\n\n#143 gives a consumer `store.listFor(userId)`.\nIt does not give a **client** anything: the browser holding the socket cannot reach the store, so\nevery consumer that wants a history sidebar has to stand up its own authenticated HTTP route over\nthe same data the engine already authenticated on the upgrade. That is a second authorisation\npath to the same personal data, written once per consumer, each one able to get the ownership\ncheck wrong in its own way — the exact failure #45 went out of its way to avoid for resume.\n\nA consumer may have no such route to extend: an engine deployment behind an api with a session\nstore and no conversation endpoints of its own has nothing to add this to. The engine already knows who is connected and already\nreplays a conversation on resume; answering \"which conversations do I have\" over the same socket\nis the smaller surface.\n\n## What to do\n\n- `src/types/messages.ts`, additive only:\n  - `ListConversationsMessage {type: 'list_conversations'; limit?: number; before?: string}` into\n    `IncomingMessage`. There is no user id field, and that is the point: the list is always the\n    connection's authenticated user's.\n  - `ConversationListMessage {type: 'conversation_list'; conversations: ConversationSummary[]}`\n    into `OutgoingMessage`, where `ConversationSummary` is `SessionSummary` with its two times as\n    ISO strings — `Date` does not survive `JSON.stringify` as a `Date`, and the protocol types\n    describe what goes on the wire.\n- `validation.ts`: a `list_conversations` case — `limit`, when present, an integer 1–200;\n  `before`, when present, a string `Date.parse` accepts. Anything else is `INVALID_MESSAGE`, as\n  the two existing cases do it.\n- Handling: `createMessageHandler` takes the session store and the new options alongside the\n  orchestrator (`createServer` already holds both), and answers `list_conversations` on the\n  non-locked path beside `ping` — a history request is not a turn and must not queue behind a\n  streaming answer.\n- The user id comes from `ws.userId`, never from the frame. Add the regression:\n  a frame carrying an extra `userId` of another user is answered with the connecting user's own\n  list.\n- Opt-in: `HalEngineConfig.transport.history?: {enabled: boolean}`, default off, threaded through\n  `createServer`'s options like `resume`. With it off, `list_conversations` is answered with an\n  `error` frame and no list.\n- A store that implements no `listFor` is answered with an `error`, not an empty list: a client\n  that renders \"no conversations\" for a user who has several is a worse answer than one that\n  renders a failure. `ErrorCodes` gains one code for both cases (`UNSUPPORTED`), additive.\n- Picking a conversation needs nothing new: the client reconnects with `?sessionId=<id>`, which\n  #45 already ships with the ownership check and the replay. No in-band switch frame, no second\n  replay path.\n- Specs and docs: extend `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md` (the two frames, their\n  field tables, the authorisation rule) and the conversation-list spec; add § Conversation list\n  to `docs/getting-started.md` by editing `example/full-config.ts` and running `npm run docs:fix`\n  — that listing sits under `<!-- doc-block: example/full-config.ts#full-config -->` and\n  `npm run docs:check` fails a hand-edited block.\n\n## Decisions\n\n- **A frame, not a REST route.** The REST chat routes are demo-only and are not where a consumer is meant to build. The WebSocket is where the caller is\n  already authenticated, once per connection, by the consumer's own `wsAuth`.\n- **Its own opt-in flag, separate from `resume`.** Resume hands a client back a conversation it\n  was already in; a list tells it what else the user has. A deployer who turned on resume did not\n  thereby agree to the second disclosure, and a config that conflates them cannot be audited.\n- **Answered off the per-session lock.** `PerSessionLock` serialises turns (#47). A list request\n  that queued behind a two-minute streaming answer would look broken, and it mutates nothing.\n- **An error, not an empty list, when it cannot be answered.** See above; silence that looks like\n  data is the one failure mode a history view cannot recover from.\n- **Answered when asked, never pushed.** No `conversation_list` arrives unsolicited: a client\n  that just started a conversation knows, and a push would need a fan-out across the sockets a\n  resumed session can be open on.\n- **The frame is accepted by `validateMessage` whether or not the feature is on**, and the\n  handler is what refuses it. Validation is static and has no config; the alternative is\n  threading options into a pure validator to turn a type into \"unknown\".\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `src/types/messages.ts` gains only new interfaces and their two union members (**amended:** four interfaces, not two — each frame needs a type for its own payload, `ConversationCursor` for what a client sends back and `ConversationSummary` for a row on the wire) — a\n      `git diff` of that file touches no existing line, and a fixture pins that every message\n      literal valid before this change still compiles\n- [ ] `validateMessage({type: 'list_conversations'})` is valid; with `limit: 0`, `limit: 201`,\n      `limit: 1.5`, `limit: '10'` or `before: 'not a date'` it is invalid with a message naming\n      the field\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` and a store holding three conversations for the user, a\n      `list_conversations` frame is answered with exactly one `conversation_list` carrying three\n      summaries, newest first, each `createdAt` and `updatedAt` an ISO string, and no\n      `stream_end`\n- [ ] A frame carrying `{type: 'list_conversations', userId: '<another user>'}` is answered with\n      the connecting user's own list, and the store's `listFor` is called with `ws.userId` —\n      asserted on the recorded argument, not only on the frame\n- [ ] `limit` and `before` reach `listFor` as the frame sent them. **Amended:** `before` is a\n      `{updatedAt, sessionId}` cursor of ISO strings, not a `Date`. This was written before\n      #143's review found that paging on a timestamp alone drops a conversation whenever two\n      share an activity time, so the store's cursor became a composite that accepts an ISO\n      string; a `Date` cannot carry the tie-break. The handler passes `{limit, before}` with\n      both possibly `undefined` rather than omitting the argument, which is the same call\n- [ ] With `history` absent, the same frame is answered with one `error` frame whose code is\n      `UNSUPPORTED` and no `conversation_list`\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` against a store implementing no `listFor`, the answer is\n      the same `error` frame, and `grep` shows no code path that sends `conversations: []`\n- [ ] A `listFor` that rejects is answered with one `error` frame whose code is `SERVER_ERROR`\n      and whose message carries no store detail, the socket stays open, and the failure is\n      logged with the session id and no user content. **Added during the build:** a store throwing\n      the new exported `StoreCannotList` is answered `UNSUPPORTED` instead, so an injected\n      collection without a `find` reads as the misconfiguration it is rather than a database\n      that failed\n- [ ] A `list_conversations` sent while a `user_message` is streaming on the same session is\n      answered before that turn's `stream_end` — it does not wait on the lock\n- [ ] A client that takes an id from a `conversation_list` and reconnects with `?sessionId=<id>`\n      receives `resumed: true` and that conversation's replay, with no new transport code: the\n      test exercises the existing #45 path end to end\n- [ ] `ConnectedMessage` is unchanged by this issue\n- [ ] **GDPR.** The protocol spec states that `conversation_list` carries one authenticated\n      user's own conversation metadata and no conversation content, that the user id is taken\n      from the connection and never from the frame, and that `transport.history` is the\n      deployer's decision to make a user's history reachable from the client; `SessionResumeOptions`'\n      retention note is mirrored on the new options type, pointing at #41\n- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry under `## [Unreleased]` written for somebody installing the\n      package\n- [ ] The `docs/getting-started.md` change is made in `example/full-config.ts` and the doc\n      regenerated with `npm run docs:fix`, not typed into the markdown\n- [ ] Spec statements carry trailing `([validated by: ...](...#Lnn))` citations, `Status` rows\n      match coverage, and `npm run check:spec-links`, `npm run check:spec-status` and\n      `npm run spec:names` pass\n- [ ] The version bump is MINOR — new optional config, two additive protocol frames, one new\n      error code — and the PR body says so; no migration guide is owed\n- [ ] `npm run verify` passes and the coverage floors for the touched groups hold or rise\n\n## Amendments\n\nThree things changed between writing this issue and building it, each marked in the criterion it\naffects:\n\n1. **`before` is a cursor, not a timestamp.** #143's review found that paging on `updatedAt` alone\n   silently drops a conversation when two share an activity time. The store's cursor became\n   `{updatedAt, sessionId}`, so the frame carries both halves and the validator refuses one half\n   alone. Built as originally written, the transport would have reintroduced the defect the store\n   had just been fixed for.\n2. **`messages.ts` gains four interfaces, not two** — the two frames plus the two payload types\n   they describe.\n3. **`StoreCannotList` is new public surface**, beyond what this issue specified: without it a\n   permanent store misconfiguration cannot be told from a transient failure, in either the client\n   frame or the log.\n\nTwo decisions the criteria did not anticipate:\n\n- **`transport.history` without `transport.resume` is useless, and now says so.** `?sessionId=` is\n  read only when resume is enabled, so a list whose rows cannot be opened is a deployment slip:\n  the engine warns at startup and the documentation says so where it tells a client to reconnect\n  with a row's id. Refusing to start was considered and rejected as too blunt for a library.\n- **The conversation the connection is in is listed like any other**, so a fresh connect sees a row\n  of its own holding no entries. Filtering it out was rejected: it is a real conversation the user\n  is in, and it is their newest the moment they send anything.\n\n## Tech notes\n\nDepends on #143: without `listFor` there is\nnothing to send.\n\nKey paths: `src/types/messages.ts`, `src/types/session.ts` (`ErrorCodes`),\n`src/transport/ws/{validation,messageHandler,sender}.ts`, `src/transport/createServer.ts`,\n`src/config.ts`, `example/full-config.ts`, `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md`.\n\nOut of scope: a preview or title on the summaries (its own issue); deleting a conversation from\nthe client, which would put an erasure primitive on the wire and needs its own authorisation\ndecision; paging state held server-side — the client pages with `before`; an operator view of\nanother user's conversations, which is an authorisation question of its own; and live sync between open tabs,\nwhich #124 ruled out.\n\nRefs #143, #145, #41, #45, #47, #124\n\n\n\n\n----\n## CI reported failures on bb3297f7d168ebf7cf74eded062b5058544fc669\n\nThe build for your last push is red. These checks failed: eslint, test, spec-names, changelog\n\nWhere a failed step is named below, run only that step's command; otherwise map each name to the job that publishes it and run only that job's command.\n\n```\n### eslint (failure)\n\nre-lint/no-duplicate-code\n   33:105  error  10 lines (73 tokens) duplicated with src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts:63-73 reported\n\n- Done: Validate list_sessions frames in src/transport/ws/validation.ts\n- Next: Thread sessionStore into createMessageHandler and handle list_sessions (session_list with ISO dates, SERVER_ERROR + ws log on failure)\n----\n# Definition of Done\n\n> #143 gives a consumer `store.listFor(userId)` inside the server process. The client on the other end of the WebSocket has no way to ask for it, and the consumer who wants a conversation picker without standing up their own REST endpoint has to write one anyway.\n\n**Strategy: `direct`** — `createServer` and the WebSocket upgrade/message pipeline already exist and accept frames over a real WebSocket connection, so acceptance tests can connect today, send `list_sessions` frames, and fail directly on the missing `session_list` and `list_sessions` validation/dispatch behaviour.\n\n## Done when these pass\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › answers list_sessions for the authenticated user with ISO dates, forwarding limit and before while ignoring any client userId** — Sends `{type: 'list_sessions', limit, before, userId}` over a connected WebSocket and verifies `store.listFor` is called with the socket's authenticated `userId` (`session.userId`) and `{limit, before}`, answering `{type: 'session_list', sessions}` with `createdAt` and `updatedAt` serialised as ISO-8601 strings.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › answers an empty session_list when the store has no listFor, and SERVER_ERROR with a ws error log when listFor throws** — Verifies that a store without `listFor` answers `{type: 'session_list', sessions: []}` and a store whose `listFor` throws/rejects logs an error under category `'ws'` with the userId and error message and answers `{type: 'error', code: 'SERVER_ERROR'}`.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › accepts a bare list_sessions frame and rejects a malformed limit or before with INVALID_MESSAGE without calling listFor** — Verifies that `{type: 'list_sessions'}` is accepted while non-integer or negative `limit` values and malformed `before` cursors (`sessionId` outside 1–128 chars or unparseable `updatedAt`) are rejected with `INVALID_MESSAGE` without invoking `store.listFor`.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n## Facets\n\n- [ ] Add `ListSessionsMessage`, `SessionSummaryFrame`, and `SessionListMessage` to `src/types/messages.ts`, include them in `IncomingMessage` and `OutgoingMessage`, and re-export them from `src/index.ts`. (`ListSessionsMessage` is in `IncomingMessage` already; `SessionSummaryFrame`, `SessionListMessage` and the `src/index.ts` re-exports remain.)\n- [x] Validate `list_sessions` frames in `src/transport/ws/validation.ts` (optional non-negative integer `limit`; optional `before` object with 1–128 char `sessionId` and finite-date `updatedAt` string or number; strip unknown fields like `userId`).\n- [ ] Thread `sessionStore` into `createMessageHandler` (optional parameter so existing 1-arg and 2-arg callers keep working) and wire it in `src/transport/createServer.ts`.\n- [ ] Handle `list_sessions` in `src/transport/ws/messageHandler.ts` (and helper in `src/transport/ws/sender.ts` if needed) by reading `session.userId`, calling `await sessionStore?.listFor?.(session.userId, {limit, before})`, serialising `Date` fields to ISO strings, and on error logging via `log.error('ws', ...)` and sending `ErrorCodes.SERVER_ERROR`.\n- [ ] Document `list_sessions` and `session_list` in `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md` and `specs/hal-engine-conversation-list/spec.md`.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- Conversation titles or previews (first user message, model-generated title).\n- Deleting or renaming a conversation from the client over the socket.\n- Switching the socket's active session in place (`?sessionId=` on reconnect is how a conversation is rejoined).\n- Holding pagination cursor state server-side (the client pages with `before`).\n- An operator view of another user's conversations or live sync between open tabs.\nbb3297f feat(transport): validate The WebSocket is where the caller is\n  already authenticated, once per connection, by the consumer's own `wsAuth`.\n- **Its own opt-in flag, separate from `resume`.** Resume hands a client back a conversation it\n  was already in; a list tells it what else the user has. A deployer who turned on resume did not\n  thereby agree to the second disclosure, and a config that conflates them cannot be audited.\n- **Answered off the per-session lock.** `PerSessionLock` serialises turns (#47). A list request\n  that queued behind a two-minute streaming answer would look broken, and it mutates nothing.\n- **An error, not an empty list, when it cannot be answered.** See above; silence that looks like\n  data is the one failure mode a history view cannot recover from.\n- **Answered when asked, never pushed.** No `conversation_list` arrives unsolicited: a client\n  that just started a conversation knows, and a push would need a fan-out across the sockets a\n  resumed session can be open on.\n- **The frame is accepted by `validateMessage` whether or not the feature is on**, and the\n  handler is what refuses it. Validation is static and has no config; the alternative is\n  threading options into a pure validator to turn a type into \"unknown\".\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `src/types/messages.ts` gains only new interfaces and their two union members (**amended:** four interfaces, not two — each frame needs a type for its own payload, `ConversationCursor` for what a client sends back and `ConversationSummary` for a row on the wire) — a\n      `git diff` of that file touches no existing line, and a fixture pins that every message\n      literal valid before this change still compiles\n- [ ] `validateMessage({type: 'list_conversations'})` is valid; with `limit: 0`, `limit: 201`,\n      `limit: 1.5`, `limit: '10'` or `before: 'not a date'` it is invalid with a message naming\n      the field\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` and a store holding three conversations for the user, a\n      `list_conversations` frame is answered with exactly one `conversation_list` carrying three\n      summaries, newest first, each `createdAt` and `updatedAt` an ISO string, and no\n      `stream_end`\n- [ ] A frame carrying `{type: 'list_conversations', userId: '<another user>'}` is answered with\n      the connecting user's own list, and the store's `listFor` is called with `ws.userId` —\n      asserted on the recorded argument, not only on the frame\n- [ ] `limit` and `before` reach `listFor` as the frame sent them. **Amended:** `before` is a\n      `{updatedAt, sessionId}` cursor of ISO strings, not a `Date`. This was written before\n      #143's review found that paging on a timestamp alone drops a conversation whenever two\n      share an activity time, so the store's cursor became a composite that accepts an ISO\n      string; a `Date` cannot carry the tie-break. The handler passes `{limit, before}` with\n      both possibly `undefined` rather than omitting the argument, which is the same call\n- [ ] With `history` absent, the same frame is answered with one `error` frame whose code is\n      `UNSUPPORTED` and no `conversation_list`\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` against a store implementing no `listFor`, the answer is\n      the same `error` frame, and `grep` shows no code path that sends `conversations: []`\n- [ ] A `listFor` that rejects is answered with one `error` frame whose code is `SERVER_ERROR`\n      and whose message carries no store detail, the socket stays open, and the failure is\n      logged with the session id and no user content. **Added during the build:** a store throwing\n      the new exported `StoreCannotList` is answered `UNSUPPORTED` instead, so an injected\n      collection without a `find` reads as the misconfiguration it is rather than a database\n      that failed\n- [ ] A `list_conversations` sent while a `user_message` is streaming on the same session is\n      answered before that turn's `stream_end` — it does not wait on the lock\n- [ ] A client that takes an id from a `conversation_list` and reconnects with `?sessionId=<id>`\n      receives `resumed: true` and that conversation's replay, with no new transport code: the\n      test exercises the existing #45 path end to end\n- [ ] `ConnectedMessage` is unchanged by this issue\n- [ ] **GDPR.** The protocol spec states that `conversation_list` carries one authenticated\n      user's own conversation metadata and no conversation content, that the user id is taken\n      from the connection and never from the frame, and that `transport.history` is the\n      deployer's decision to make a user's history reachable from the client; `SessionResumeOptions`'\n      retention note is mirrored on the new options type, pointing at #41\n- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry under `## [Unreleased]` written for somebody installing the\n      package\n- [ ] The `docs/getting-started.md` change is made in `example/full-config.ts` and the doc\n      regenerated with `npm run docs:fix`, not typed into the markdown\n- [ ] Spec statements carry trailing `([validated by: ...](...#Lnn))` citations, `Status` rows\n      match coverage, and `npm run check:spec-links`, `npm run check:spec-status` and\n      `npm run spec:names` pass\n- [ ] The version bump is MINOR — new optional config, two additive protocol frames, one new\n      error code — and the PR body says so; no migration guide is owed\n- [ ] `npm run verify` passes and the coverage floors for the touched groups hold or rise\n\n## Amendments\n\nThree things changed between writing this issue and building it, each marked in the criterion it\naffects:\n\n1. **`before` is a cursor, not a timestamp.** #143's review found that paging on `updatedAt` alone\n   silently drops a conversation when two share an activity time. The store's cursor became\n   `{updatedAt, sessionId}`, so the frame carries both halves and the validator refuses one half\n   alone. Built as originally written, the transport would have reintroduced the defect the store\n   had just been fixed for.\n2. **`messages.ts` gains four interfaces, not two** — the two frames plus the two payload types\n   they describe.\n3. **`StoreCannotList` is new public surface**, beyond what this issue specified: without it a\n   permanent store misconfiguration cannot be told from a transient failure, in either the client\n   frame or the log.\n\nTwo decisions the criteria did not anticipate:\n\n- **`transport.history` without `transport.resume` is useless, and now says so.** `?sessionId=` is\n  read only when resume is enabled, so a list whose rows cannot be opened is a deployment slip:\n  the engine warns at startup and the documentation says so where it tells a client to reconnect\n  with a row's id. Refusing to start was considered and rejected as too blunt for a library.\n- **The conversation the connection is in is listed like any other**, so a fresh connect sees a row\n  of its own holding no entries. Filtering it out was rejected: it is a real conversation the user\n  is in, and it is their newest the moment they send anything.\n\n## Tech notes\n\nDepends on #143: without `listFor` there is\nnothing to send.\n\nKey paths: `src/types/messages.ts`, `src/types/session.ts` (`ErrorCodes`),\n`src/transport/ws/{validation,messageHandler,sender}.ts`, `src/transport/createServer.ts`,\n`src/config.ts`, `example/full-config.ts`, `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md`.\n\nOut of scope: a preview or title on the summaries (its own issue); deleting a conversation from\nthe client, which would put an erasure primitive on the wire and needs its own authorisation\ndecision; paging state held server-side — the client pages with `before`; an operator view of\nanother user's conversations, which is an authorisation question of its own; and live sync between open tabs,\nwhich #124 ruled out.\n\nRefs #143, #145, #41, #45, #47, #124\n\n\n\n\n----\n## CI reported failures on bb3297f7d168ebf7cf74eded062b5058544fc669\n\nThe build for your last push is red. These checks failed: eslint, test, spec-names, changelog\n\nWhere a failed step is named below, run only that step's command; otherwise map each name to the job that publishes it and run only that {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CYXgXNfSVfwKy8J3QMbrGy","type":"tool_result","content":"#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Fails when a diff touches src/ and leaves CHANGELOG.md alone.\n#\n# Usage:\n#   bash scripts/check-changelog.sh [base-ref]     # default: origin/main\n#\n# The rule is about what a consumer can observe, not about effort: anything that\n# changes shipped behaviour needs a sentence they can read on npm. A change to\n# src/ that genuinely is not user-visible - an internal refactor, a test-only\n# edit - takes the `no-changelog` label instead, which is a deliberate, visible\n# act rather than a silent omission.\nset -uo pipefail\n\nbase=\"${1:-origin/main}\"\n\n# A base ref that does not resolve means nothing was compared. On a laptop that\n# is ordinary - a fresh clone has no origin/main - and skipping is right. In CI\n# it is the gate passing without running, which is the one outcome a gate must\n# never have, and it is exactly how a fetch that failed went unnoticed.\nif ! git rev-parse --verify --quiet \"$base\" >/dev/null; then\n  if [ -n \"${GITHUB_ACTIONS:-}\" ]; then\n    echo \"check-changelog: FAILED - base ref '$base' does not resolve, so nothing was checked\" >&2\n    exit 1\n  fi\n  echo \"check-changelog: base ref '$base' not found; skipping\" >&2\n  exit 0\nfi\n\nmerge_base=\"$(git merge-base \"$base\" HEAD)\"\nchanged=\"$(git diff --name-only \"$merge_base\"..HEAD)\"\n\nsrc_changed=\"$(printf '%s\\n' \"$changed\" | grep -E '^src/' | grep -vE '\\.test\\.ts$' || true)\"\n\nif [ -z \"$src_changed\" ]; then\n  echo \"check-changelog: no shipped source changed since ${base}.\"\n  exit 0\nfi\n\nif printf '%s\\n' \"$changed\" | grep -qx 'CHANGELOG.md'; then\n  echo \"check-changelog: src/ changed and CHANGELOG.md was updated.\"\ncheck-spec-anchor-names: specs/hal-engine-conversation-list/spec.md: names \"answers list_sessions for the authenticated user with ISO dates, forwarding limit and before while ignoring any client userId\" but points at #L87; it is at #L93\ncheck-spec-anchor-names: specs/hal-engine-conversation-list/spec.md: names \"answers an empty session_list when the store has no listFor, and SERVER_ERROR with a ws error log when listFor throws\" but points at #L129; it is at #L134\ncheck-spec-anchor-names: specs/hal-engine-conversation-list/spec.md: names \"accepts a bare list_sessions frame and rejects a malformed limit or before with INVALID_MESSAGE without calling listFor\" but points at #L161; it is at #L163\n\ncheck-spec-anchor-names: 3 finding(s).\nRun `npm run spec:names -- --fix` to repoint a citation from the name it carries.\n# Changelog\n\nAll notable changes to `@re-cinq/hal-engine` are documented here.\n\nThe format is [Keep a Changelog 1.1.0](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and this project adheres to\n[Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html). Entries are written for somebody installing the\npackage, not for somebody reading this repository's commit log.\n\n## [Unreleased]\n\n### Added\n\n- `SessionStore.listFor(userId, options?)`, an optional store member that answers the summaries of a user's conversations — `sessionId`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt` and `entryCount` — most recent activity first, so a consumer can build a conversation list instead of only rejoining one. Nothing in the engine calls it yet: the store answers the list, and the route or frame that carries it to a client is still yours to write. `InMemorySessionStore` and `MongoSessionStore` both implement it; a store of your own may leave it out. A summary never carries a conversation's entries or its credentials. Page with `limit` (50 by default, 200 at most, and `0` answers nothing) and `before`, which takes the last summary you saw: the cursor carries both `updatedAt` and `sessionId`, so two conversations saved in the same millisecond cannot straddle a page boundary and go unlisted. See `docs/session-stores.md` § Listing a user's conversations.\n\n### Changed\n\n- `MongoSessionStore` now writes an `entryCount` field beside the entries on every save, so a conversation list can count a conversation without reading one. A document saved by an earlier version lists as a conversation of no entries until you backfill it; `docs/session-stores.md` carries the one-line command.\n- `MongoSessionStore.latestFor` now breaks a tie on `updatedAt` by `_id`, so it and `listFor` agree on which conversation is newest when two were saved in the same millisecond. Which of two tied conversations a resume rejoins can therefore change.\n- The index to create for `MongoSessionStore` is now `{userId: 1, updatedAt: -1, _id: -1}`. The two-key form that served `latestFor` cannot satisfy `listFor`'s sort, so Mongo would sort a user's whole history on every page. `docs/session-stores.md` § Latest session has the command.\n- `CollectionLike` gains an optional `find`, and `CursorLike`, `FindManyOptions` and `FindOneOptions` are now exported for anyone writing their own adapter. A collection without `find` still satisfies the type; `listFor` refuses on it rather than reporting that the user has no conversations.\n\n## [0.5.0] - 2026-10-04\n\n**Upgrading from 0.4.x.** One thing is removed, and several things an existing consumer observes change. Check these before you bump:\n\n- Every client must offer `hal.v1` beside its token, `new WebSocket(url, ['hal.v1', token])`. A bare-token offer, accepted with a deprecation warning since 0.3.0, now fails to connect, and `credentialFromSubprotocol` returns `undefined` for it.\n- Closing a socket abandons its turn: the run stops reading from the model and abandons its tool calls, no `stream_end` follows the close, and `afterModelResponse` and `onError` do not fire for it, while `afterSession` still does. A missed heartbeat and `stop()` close sockets too, so drain connections before a deploy.\n- Every tool call now has 30 seconds to settle, a `beforeToolCall` policy included. A tool that legitimately runs longer needs a larger `toolTimeoutMs`, or `0` for the old unbounded wait.\n- `ToolRegistry.execute` no longer throws for a tool name the model invented, or rejects when an executor throws: the model is answered instead. It throws a `TypeError` for an input that is not an object, whatever the tool's schema.\n- Messages in one session are answered one at a time, so a message sent while another is streaming starts after that one's `stream_end`.","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"926ccd8c-95d3-4391-85f8-d038c835401a","uuid":"42119213-fa1f-4050-9dc7-1214b5c696d3","timestamp":"2026-11-06T16:27:47.489Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Fails when a diff touches src/ and leaves CHANGELOG.md alone.\n#\n# Usage:\n#   bash scripts/check-changelog.sh [base-ref]     # default: origin/main\n#\n# The rule is about what a consumer can observe, not about effort: anything that\n# changes shipped behaviour needs a sentence they can read on npm. A change to\n# src/ that genuinely is not user-visible - an internal refactor, a test-only\n# edit - takes the `no-changelog` label instead, which is a deliberate, visible\n# act rather than a silent omission.\nset -uo pipefail\n\nbase=\"${1:-origin/main}\"\n\n# A base ref that does not resolve means nothing was compared. On a laptop that\n# is ordinary - a fresh clone has no origin/main - and skipping is right. In CI\n# it is the gate passing without running, which is the one outcome a gate must\n# never have, and it is exactly how a fetch that failed went unnoticed.\nif ! git rev-parse --verify --quiet \"$base\" >/dev/null; then\n  if [ -n \"${GITHUB_ACTIONS:-}\" ]; then\n    echo \"check-changelog: FAILED - base ref '$base' does not resolve, so nothing was checked\" >&2\n    exit 1\n  fi\n  echo \"check-changelog: base ref '$base' not found; skipping\" >&2\n  exit 0\nfi\n\nmerge_base=\"$(git merge-base \"$base\" HEAD)\"\nchanged=\"$(git diff --name-only \"$merge_base\"..HEAD)\"\n\nsrc_changed=\"$(printf '%s\\n' \"$changed\" | grep -E '^src/' | grep -vE '\\.test\\.ts$' || true)\"\n\nif [ -z \"$src_changed\" ]; then\n  echo \"check-changelog: no shipped source changed since ${base}.\"\n  exit 0\nfi\n\nif printf '%s\\n' \"$changed\" | grep -qx 'CHANGELOG.md'; then\n  echo \"check-changelog: src/ changed and CHANGELOG.md was updated.\"\ncheck-spec-anchor-names: specs/hal-engine-conversation-list/spec.md: names \"answers list_sessions for the authenticated user with ISO dates, forwarding limit and before while ignoring any client userId\" but points at #L87; it is at #L93\ncheck-spec-anchor-names: specs/hal-engine-conversation-list/spec.md: names \"answers an empty session_list when the store has no listFor, and SERVER_ERROR with a ws error log when listFor throws\" but points at #L129; it is at #L134\ncheck-spec-anchor-names: specs/hal-engine-conversation-list/spec.md: names \"accepts a bare list_sessions frame and rejects a malformed limit or before with INVALID_MESSAGE without calling listFor\" but points at #L161; it is at #L163\n\ncheck-spec-anchor-names: 3 finding(s).\nRun `npm run spec:names -- --fix` to repoint a citation from the name it carries.\n# Changelog\n\nAll notable changes to `@re-cinq/hal-engine` are documented here.\n\nThe format is [Keep a Changelog 1.1.0](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and this project adheres to\n[Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html). Entries are written for somebody installing the\npackage, not for somebody reading this repository's commit log.\n\n## [Unreleased]\n\n### Added\n\n- `SessionStore.listFor(userId, options?)`, an optional store member that answers the summaries of a user's conversations — `sessionId`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt` and `entryCount` — most recent activity first, so a consumer can build a conversation list instead of only rejoining one. Nothing in the engine calls it yet: the store answers the list, and the route or frame that carries it to a client is still yours to write. `InMemorySessionStore` and `MongoSessionStore` both implement it; a store of your own may leave it out. A summary never carries a conversation's entries or its credentials. Page with `limit` (50 by default, 200 at most, and `0` answers nothing) and `before`, which takes the last summary you saw: the cursor carries both `updatedAt` and `sessionId`, so two conversations saved in the same millisecond cannot straddle a page boundary and go unlisted. See `docs/session-stores.md` § Listing a user's conversations.\n\n### Changed\n\n- `MongoSessionStore` now writes an `entryCount` field beside the entries on every save, so a conversation list can count a conversation without reading one. A document saved by an earlier version lists as a conversation of no entries until you backfill it; `docs/session-stores.md` carries the one-line command.\n- `MongoSessionStore.latestFor` now breaks a tie on `updatedAt` by `_id`, so it and `listFor` agree on which conversation is newest when two were saved in the same millisecond. Which of two tied conversations a resume rejoins can therefore change.\n- The index to create for `MongoSessionStore` is now `{userId: 1, updatedAt: -1, _id: -1}`. The two-key form that served `latestFor` cannot satisfy `listFor`'s sort, so Mongo would sort a user's whole history on every page. `docs/session-stores.md` § Latest session has the command.\n- `CollectionLike` gains an optional `find`, and `CursorLike`, `FindManyOptions` and `FindOneOptions` are now exported for anyone writing their own adapter. A collection without `find` still satisfies the type; `listFor` refuses on it rather than reporting that the user has no conversations.\n\n## [0.5.0] - 2026-10-04\n\n**Upgrading from 0.4.x.** One thing is removed, and several things an existing consumer observes change. Check these before you bump:\n\n- Every client must offer `hal.v1` beside its token, `new WebSocket(url, ['hal.v1', token])`. A bare-token offer, accepted with a deprecation warning since 0.3.0, now fails to connect, and `credentialFromSubprotocol` returns `undefined` for it.\n- Closing a socket abandons its turn: the run stops reading from the model and abandons its tool calls, no `stream_end` follows the close, and `afterModelResponse` and `onError` do not fire for it, while `afterSession` still does. A missed heartbeat and `stop()` close sockets too, so drain connections before a deploy.\n- Every tool call now has 30 seconds to settle, a `beforeToolCall` policy included. A tool that legitimately runs longer needs a larger `toolTimeoutMs`, or `0` for the old unbounded wait.\n- `ToolRegistry.execute` no longer throws for a tool name the model invented, or rejects when an executor throws: the model is answered instead. It throws a `TypeError` for an input that is not an object, whatever the tool's schema.\n- Messages in one session are answered one at a time, so a message sent while another is streaming starts after that one's `stream_end`.","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01CYXgXNfSVfwKy8J3QMbrGy","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01SMb4KRuTKSTZpYjx8M2dHL","type":"tool_result","content":"eslint.config.backlog.mjs\neslint.config.mjs\neslint.config.mjs:88:      're-lint/no-duplicate-code': ['error', {ignore: ['**/*.md']}],\neslint.config.mjs:110:      're-lint/prefer-early-return': 'error',\nconst suppressingMidAnswer: ChatOrchestrator = {\n  processMessage: async () => 'before after',\n  async *processMessageStream(): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n    yield {type: 'text', text: 'before'};\n    yield {type: 'suppress_output'} as unknown as MessageChunk;\n    yield {type: 'text', text: ' after'};\n    yield {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'end_turn'};\n  },\n};\n\nconst servers: Array<ReturnType<typeof createServer>> = [];\nconst clients: WebSocket[] = [];\n\nconst eventually = async (check: () => boolean): Promise<void> => {\n  for (let attempt = 0; attempt < 50 && !check(); attempt++) await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 10));\n};\n\n// Silenced for the whole file: the server logs every connection and close, and these tests read frames, not logs.\nbeforeAll(() => {\n  setLogger({debug: () => undefined, info: () => undefined, warn: () => undefined, error: () => undefined});\n});\n\nafterAll(() => {\n  setLogger();\n});\n\n// Each server sees its sockets close before it stops, so no close is logged after the logger above is restored.\nafterEach(async () => {\n  for (const client of clients.splice(0)) client.terminate();\n  for (const hal of servers.splice(0)) {\n    const {wss} = hal;\n    await eventually(() => wss.clients.size === 0);\n    await hal.stop();\n  }\n});\n\n// The authenticated user is read per upgrade, so one test can reconnect as someone else.\nconst startEngine = async (\n  store: SessionStore,\n  resume?: SessionResumeOptions,\n  orchestrator: ChatOrchestrator = answeringOrchestrator\n// Each stored conversation holds one user entry, sent at the given time and reading as its own id.\nconst storeWith = (conversations: Array<[sessionId: string, userId: string, sentAt: string]>): InMemorySessionStore => {\n  const store = new InMemorySessionStore();\n  for (const [sessionId, userId, sentAt] of conversations) {\n    const {entries} = store.create(sessionId, userId);\n    entries.push({role: 'user', content: sessionId, timestamp: sentAt});\n  }\n  return store;\n};\n\n// A store with only the required members, so an optional one can be added, replaced or left out per test.\nconst delegatingTo = (inner: InMemorySessionStore): SessionStore => ({\n  create: (sessionId, userId, options) => inner.create(sessionId, userId, options),\n  get: sessionId => inner.get(sessionId),\n  delete: sessionId => inner.delete(sessionId),\n  count: () => inner.count(),\n  clear: () => inner.clear(),\n});\n\ndescribe('resuming a conversation on reconnect', () => {\n  it('with resume absent, answers a reconnect naming the first id with a new id and no replay', async () => {\n    const {base} = await startEngine(new InMemorySessionStore());\n    const first = await firstTurn(base);\n\n    const frames = await connectAndSettle(`${base}?sessionId=${first}`);\n\nimport {validateMessage} from './validation.js';\n\n// One wire name for the client frame: the alias is gone, and the frame the validator returns is the exported shape.\n\ndescribe('the websocket frame validator', () => {\n  it('accepts user_message and returns the frame the exported union describes', () => {\n    const result = validateMessage({type: 'user_message', content: 'hello'});\n\n    expect(result).toEqual({valid: true, data: {type: 'user_message', content: 'hello'}});\n  });\n\n  it('rejects send_message, which is no longer a wire name', () => {\n    const result = validateMessage({type: 'send_message', content: 'hello'});\n\n    expect(result).toEqual({valid: false, error: 'Unknown message type: send_message'});\n  });\n\n  it('drops any field the frame does not define, rather than forwarding it', () => {\n    const result = validateMessage({type: 'user_message', content: 'hello', chatId: 'c1'});\n\n    expect(result).toEqual({valid: true, data: {type: 'user_message', content: 'hello'}});\n  });\n\n  it('accepts list_sessions with only limit and before, and rejects a malformed limit or before cursor', () => {\n    const before = {updatedAt: '2026-01-01T00:05:00.000Z', sessionId: 's3'};\n    const malformed = [\n      {type: 'list_sessions', limit: 'ten'},\n      {type: sessionId: ''}},\n      {type: 'list_sessions', before: {updatedAt: before.updatedAt, sessionId: 'a'.repeat(129)}},\n      {type: 'list_sessions', before: {updatedAt: 'not-a-date', sessionId: 's1'}},\n      {type: 'list_sessions', before: {updatedAt: true, sessionId: 's1'}},\n    ];\n\n    expect({\n      bare: validateMessage({type: 'list_sessions'}),\n      full: validateMessage({type: 'list_sessions', limit: 10, before: {...before, extra: 1}, userId: 'u2'}),\n      epoch: validateMessage({type: 'list_sessions', before: {updatedAt: 1767225900000, sessionId: 's3'}}),\n      malformed: malformed.map(frame => validateMessage(frame).valid),\n    }).toEqual({\n      bare: {valid: true, data: {type: 'list_sessions'}},\n      full: {valid: true, data: {type: 'list_sessions', limit: 10, before}},\n      epoch: {valid: true, data: {type: 'list_sessions', before: {updatedAt: 1767225900000, sessionId: a/specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md\n+++ b/specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md\n@@ -160,6 +160,26 @@ Application-level heartbeat. The server MUST respond with a `pong` message echoi\n | `type`      | string | Yes      | `\"ping\"`                          |\n | `timestamp` | number | Yes      | Client timestamp in milliseconds  |\n \n+### 4.3 list_sessions\n+\n+Asks for the conversations of the connection's authenticated user, newest activity first.\n+\n+```json\n+{\n+  \"type\": \"list_sessions\",\n+  \"limit\": 20,\n+  \"before\": {\"updatedAt\": \"2026-01-01T00:05:00.000Z\", \"sessionId\": \"s3\"}\n+}\n+```\n+\n+| Field    | Type   | Required | Description                                                          |\n+| -------- | ------ | -------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n+| `type`   | string | Yes      | `\"list_sessions\"`                                                    |\n+| `limit`  | number | No       | Non-negative integer: the most rows to return                        |\n+| `before` | object | No       | Cursor `{updatedAt, sessionId}` from the last row of a previous page |\n+\n+The validator forwards `limit` and `before` and nothing else; a `userId` on the frame is dropped, since the user is the connection's. A `limit` that is not a non-negative integer, or a `before` that is not an object whose `updatedAt` is a string or number naming a valid date and whose `sessionId` is a string of 1–128 characters, is answered with `INVALID_MESSAGE` ([validated by: accepts list_sessions with only limit and before, and rejects a malformed limit or before cursor](../../src/transport/ws/validation.test.ts#L24)).\n+\n ## 5. limit !== 'number' || !Number.isInteger(limit) || limit < 0) {\n+      return {valid: false, error: 'list_sessions limit must be a non-negative integer'};\n+    }\n+    data.limit = limit;\n+  }\n+\n+ undefined;\n+  if (!Number.isFinite(new Date(updatedAt).getTime())) return undefined;\n+  if (typeof sessionId !== 'string' || sessionId.length < 1 || sessionId.length > MAX_SESSION_ID_LENGTH)\n+ '../../types/ai.js';\nimport {AIError} from '../../types/ai.js';\nimport type {ChatSession, SessionEntry} from '../../types/session.js';\nimport type {OutgoingMessage} from '../../types/messages.js';\nimport {PerSessionLock} from '../../shared/perSessionLock.js';\nimport {deferred} from '../wsTestSupport.js';\n\n// The frames, in order, are the contract - above all for suppression, which retracts.\n\n// One line per frame; entries carry generated timestamps no assertion should read.\nconst wire = (sent: OutgoingMessage[]): string[] =>\n  sent.map(frame => {\n    if (frame.type === 'entry_upsert') {\n      const content = 'content' in frame.entry ? frame.entry.content : '';\n      return `upsert ${frame.index} ${frame.entry.role} ${JSON.stringify(content)}`;\n    }\n    if (frame.type === 'entry_delta') return `delta ${frame.index} ${JSON.stringify(frame.delta)}`;\n    if (frame.type === 'entry_commit') return `commit ${frame.index}`;\n    if (frame.type === 'entry_skip') return `skip ${frame.index}`;\n    if (frame.type === 'error') return `error ${frame.code}`;\n    if (frame.type === 'pong') return `pong ${frame.timestamp}`;\n    return frame.type;\n  });\n\nconst text = (value: string): MessageChunk => ({type: 'text', text: value});\nconst STOP: MessageChunk = {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'end_turn'};\nconst SUPPRESS = {type: 'suppress_output'} as unknown as MessageChunk;\n\ninterface StreamScript {\n  failWith?: Error;\n  pauseAfter?: {chunks: number; until: Promise<void>};\n}\n\nconst harness = (chunks: MessageChunk[], {failWith, pauseAfter}: StreamScript = {}) => {\n  const sent: OutgoingMessage[] = [];\n  const ws = {send: (raw: string) => sent.push(JSON.parse(raw) as OutgoingMessage)} as unknown as WebSocket;\n\n  const stream = async function* (): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n    for (const [position, chunk] of chunks.entries()) {\n      if (pauseAfter && position === pauseAfter.chunks) await pauseAfter.until;\n      yield chunk;\n    }\n    if (failWith) throw failWith;\n  };\n\n  const processMessageStream = jest.fn(stream);\n  const orchestrator = {processMessageStream} as unknown as ChatOrchestrator;\n  const session: ChatSession = {sessionId: 's1', userId: 'u1', entries: []};\n\n  const handle = createMessageHandler(orchestrator);\n\n  return {\n    session,\n    processMessageStream,\n    sent,\n    frames: () => wire(sent),\n    send: (raw: unknown = {type: 'user_message', content: 'hello'}, signal?: AbortSignal) =>\n      handle(ws, session, raw, signal),\n  };\n};\n\ndescribe('the websocket message handler', () => {\n  describe('dispatch', () => {\n    it('rejects an unparseable message and never starts a stream', async () => {\n      const h = harness([]);\n\n      await h.send({type: 'nonsense'});\n\n      const {calls} = h.processMessageStream.mock;\n\n      expect({frames: h.frames(), streams: calls.length}).toEqual({frames: ['error INVALID_MESSAGE'], streams: 0});\n    });\n\n    it('answers a ping with the timestamp it was given', async () => {\n      const h = harness([]);\n\n      await h.send({type: 'ping', timestamp: 1234});\n\n      expect(h.frames()).toEqual(['pong 1234']);\n    });\n  });\n\n  describe('a plain assistant reply', () => {\n    it('sends the user entry, then the assistant entry, its delta, its commit and stream_end', async () => {\n      const h = harness([text('hi there'), STOP]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      expect(h.frames()).toEqual([\n        'upsert 0 user \"hello\"',\n        'upsert 1 assistant \"\"',\n        'delta 1 \"hi there\"',\n        'commit 1',\n        'stream_end',\n      ]);\n    });\n\n    it('leaves the committed assistant entry in the session, no longer streaming', async () => {\n      const h = harness([text('hi there'), STOP]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      const {entries} = h.session;\n\n      expect(entries[1]).toMatchObject({role: 'assistant', content: 'hi there', isStreaming: false});\n    });\n  });\n\n  describe('thinking segments', () => {\n    it('commits the thinking entry before the assistant entry opens', async () => {\n      const h = harness([text('<thinking>pondering</thinking>answer'), STOP]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      expect(h.frames()).toEqual([\n        'upsert 0 user \"hello\"',\n        'upsert 1 thinking \"\"',\n        'delta 1 \"pondering\"',\n        'commit 1',\n        'upsert 2 assistant \"\"',\n        'delta 2 \"answer\"',\n        'commit 2',\n        'stream_end',\n      ]);\n    });\n\n    it('commits the thinking entry when text resumes, not when a tool entry appears', async () => {\n      const h = harness([\n        text('<thinking>I should look up the weather for Berlin.</thinking>'),\n        {type: 'tool_use', toolCall: {id: 'c1', name: 'get_weather', input: {location: 'Berlin'}}} as MessageChunk,\n        text('Berlin currently has '),\n        text('a temperature of 18 degrees Celsius with clear skies.'),\n        STOP,\n      ]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      expect(h.frames()).toEqual([\n        'upsert 0 user \"hello\"',\n        'upsert 1 thinking \"\"',\n        'delta 1 \"I should look up the weather for Berlin.\"',\n        'upsert 2 tool \"\"',\n        'commit 1',\n        'upsert 3 assistant \"\"',\n        'delta 3 \"Berlin currently has \"',\n        'delta 3 \"a temperature of 18 degrees Celsius with clear skies.\"',\n        'commit 3',\n        'stream_end',\n      ]);\n    });\n  });\n\n  describe('tool chunks', () => {\n    it('sends a tool entry for a tool call', async () => {\n      const h = harness([{type: 'tool_use', toolCall: {id: 'c1', name: 'lookup', input: {q: 1}}}, STOP]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      expect(h.frames()).toEqual(['upsert 0 user \"hello\"', 'upsert 1 tool \"\"', 'stream_end']);\n    });\n\n    it('forwards a tool result to the client untouched', async () => {\n      const forwarded = {type: 'entry_commit', index: 41} as OutgoingMessage;\n      const h = harness([{type: 'tool_result', clientMessages: [forwarded]} as MessageChunk, STOP]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      expect(h.frames()).toEqual(['upsert 0 user \"hello\"', 'commit 41', 'stream_end']);\n    });\n  });\n\n  describe('suppression', () => {\n    it('blanks an assistant entry that was already sent', async () => {\n      const h = harness([text('leaked'), STOP, SUPPRESS]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      expect(h.frames()).toEqual([\n        'upsert 0 user \"hello\"',\n        'upsert 1 assistant \"\"',\n        'delta 1 \"leaked\"',\n        'commit 1',\n        'upsert 1 assistant \"\"',\n        'stream_end',\n      ]);\n    });\n\n    it('never blanks a thinking entry, only assistant ones', async () => {\n      const h = harness([text('<thinking>private</thinking>said'), STOP, SUPPRESS]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      expect(h.frames().filter(frame => frame.startsWith('upsert 1'))).toEqual(['upsert 1 thinking \"\"']);\n    });\n\n    it('skips rather than upserts a segment opened after suppression', async () => {\n      const h = harness([SUPPRESS, text('hidden'), STOP]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      expect(h.frames()).toEqual(['upsert 0 user \"hello\"', 'skip 1', 'stream_end']);\n    });\n\n    it('still records a suppressed reply in the session, so history keeps it', async () => {\n      const h = harness([SUPPRESS, text('hidden'), STOP]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      const {entries} = h.session;\n\n      expect(entries[1]).toMatchObject({role: 'assistant', content: 'hidden', isStreaming: false});\n    });\n\n    it('does not resurrect an entry the client only ever saw skipped', async () => {\n      const h = harness([SUPPRESS, text('hidden'), STOP, SUPPRESS]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      expect(h.frames()).toEqual(['upsert 0 user \"hello\"', 'skip 1', 'stream_end']);\n    });\n\n    it('blanks each sent entry once, so a second suppression repeats nothing', async () => {\n      const h = harness([text('leaked'), STOP, SUPPRESS, SUPPRESS]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      expect(h.frames().filter(frame => frame === 'upsert 1 assistant \"\"')).toHaveLength(2);\n    });\n\n    it('marks a sent assistant entry it blanked as suppressed in the session, keeping its text', async () => {\n      const h = harness([text('leaked'), STOP, SUPPRESS]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      const [, answer] = h.session.entries;\n      expect(answer).toMatchObject({role: 'assistant', content: 'leaked', suppressed: true});\n    });\n\n    it('marks a segment opened after suppression as suppressed in the session', async () => {\n      const h = harness([SUPPRESS, text('hidden'), STOP]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      const [, answer] = h.session.entries;\n      expect(answer).toMatchObject({role: 'assistant', content: 'hidden', suppressed: true});\n    });\n\n    it('leaves a thinking entry the client kept seeing unmarked, and marks the blanked answer', async () => {\n      const h = harness([text('<thinking>private</thinking>said'), STOP, SUPPRESS]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      const [, thinking, answer] = h.session.entries;\n      expect({thinking: 'suppressed' in thinking, answer}).toMatchObject({\n        thinking: false,\n        answer: {role: 'assistant', content: 'said', suppressed: true},\n      });\n    });\n\n    it('commits and retracts an answer still open when suppression begins, and skips what follows as a new entry', async () => {\n      const h = harness([text('before'), SUPPRESS, text(' after'), STOP]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      const [, answer, after] = h.session.entries;\n      expect({frames: h.frames(), answer, after}).toMatchObject({\n        frames: [\n          'upsert 0 user \"hello\"',\n          'upsert 1 assistant \"\"',\n          'delta 1 \"before\"',\n          'commit 1',\n          'upsert 1 assistant \"\"',\n          'skip 2',\n          'stream_end',\n        ],\n        answer: {role: 'assistant', content: 'before', isStreaming: false, suppressed: true},\n        after: {role: 'assistant', content: ' after', suppressed: true},\n      });\n    });\n\n    it('commits a thought still open when suppression begins as the client saw it, and skips what follows', async () => {\n      const h = harness([text('<thinking>early'), SUPPRESS, text(' later</thinking>'), STOP]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      const [, thought, after] = h.session.entries;\n      expect({frames: h.frames(), thought, marked: 'suppressed' in thought, after}).toMatchObject({\n        frames: [\n          'upsert 0 user \"hello\"',\n          'upsert 1 thinking \"\"',\n          'delta 1 \"early\"',\n          'commit 1',\n          'skip 2',\n          'stream_end',\n        ],\n        thought: {role: 'thinking', content: 'early', isStreaming: false},\n        marked: false,\n        after: {role: 'thinking', content: ' later', suppressed: true},\n      });\n    });\n  });\n\n  describe('failures', () => {\n    it('tells the client to retry when the provider is rate limited', async () => {\n      // eslint-disable-next-line re-lint/no-flag-params -- AIError's retryable flag; see adrs/ADR-006-lint-suppressions.md\n      const h = harness([], {failWith: new AIError('slow down', 'RATE_LIMITED', true)});\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      expect(h.frames()).toEqual(['upsert 0 user \"hello\"', 'error RATE_LIMITED', 'stream_end']);\n    });\n\n    it('reports any other failure as a server error', async () => {\n      const h = harness([], {failWith: new Error('boom')});\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      expect(h.frames()).toEqual(['upsert 0 user \"hello\"', 'error SERVER_ERROR', 'stream_end']);\n    });\n  });\n\n  describe('the terminal frame', () => {\n    it('ends a provider failure mid-stream with error then stream_end, stream_end last', async () => {\n      const h = harness([text('Half an ans')], {failWith: new Error('provider dropped')});\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      expect(h.frames()).toEqual([\n        'upsert 0 user \"hello\"',\n        'upsert 1 assistant \"\"',\n        'delta 1 \"Half an ans\"',\n        'upsert 1 assistant \"Half an ans\"',\n        'commit 1',\n        'error SERVER_ERROR',\n        'stream_end',\n      ]);\n    });\n\n    const invalidRun = async (content: string) => {\n      const h = harness([]);\n      await h.send({type: 'user_message', content});\n      const {calls} = h.processMessageStream.mock;\n      return {frames: h.frames(), streams: calls.length};\n    };\n    const REFUSED = {frames: ['error INVALID_MESSAGE', 'stream_end'], streams: 0};\n\n    it('answers an empty user_message with INVALID_MESSAGE then stream_end', async () => {\n      expect(await invalidRun('')).toEqual(REFUSED);\n    });\n\n    it('answers a blank user_message with INVALID_MESSAGE then stream_end', async () => {\n      expect(await invalidRun('   ')).toEqual(REFUSED);\n    });\n\n    it('answers an over-length user_message with INVALID_MESSAGE then stream_end', async () => {\n      expect(await invalidRun('x'.repeat(10_001))).toEqual(REFUSED);\n    });\n\n    it('answers a malformed ping with INVALID_MESSAGE and no stream_end', async () => {\n      const h = harness([]);\n\n      await h.send({type: 'ping', timestamp: 'soon'});\n\n      expect(h.frames()).toEqual(['error INVALID_MESSAGE']);\n    });\n\n    it('keeps one stream_end, last, when a malformed ping lands mid-run', async () => {\n      const gate = deferred();\n      const h = harness([text('first '), text('second'), STOP], {pauseAfter: {chunks: 1, until: gate.until}});\n\n      const run = h.send();\n      await new Promise(resolve => setImmediate(resolve));\n      await h.send({type: 'ping', timestamp: 'soon'});\n      gate.release();\n      await run;\n\n      expect(h.frames()).toEqual([\n        'upsert 0 user \"hello\"',\n        'upsert 1 assistant \"\"',\n        'delta 1 \"first \"',\n        'error INVALID_MESSAGE',\n        'delta 1 \"second\"',\n        'commit 1',\n        'stream_end',\n      ]);\n    });\n\n    it('sends one stream_end for a three-round tool conversation', async () => {\n      const call = (id: string): MessageChunk => ({type: 'tool_use', toolCall: {id, name: 'lookup', input: {id}}});\n      const h = harness([call('c1'), call('c2'), call('c3'), text('Done'), STOP]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      const frames = h.frames();\n      expect({ends: frames.filter(frame => frame === 'stream_end').length, last: frames.at(-1)}).toEqual({\n        ends: 1,\n        last: 'stream_end',\n      });\n    });\n  });\n\n  describe('open entries on every terminal path', () => {\n    const stillStreaming = ({entries}: ChatSession): number =>\n      entries.filter(entry => 'isStreaming' in entry && entry.isStreaming).length;\n    const DROPPED = new Error('provider dropped');\n\n    it('commits a partial answer as streamed, before the error, when the provider throws', async () => {\n      const h = harness([text('partial answer')], {failWith: DROPPED});\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      const [, answer] = h.session.entries;\n      expect({frames: h.frames(), open: stillStreaming(h.session), answer}).toMatchObject({\n        frames: [\n          'upsert 0 user \"hello\"',\n          'upsert 1 assistant \"\"',\n          'delta 1 \"partial answer\"',\n          'upsert 1 assistant \"partial answer\"',\n          'commit 1',\n          'error SERVER_ERROR',\n          'stream_end',\n        ],\n        open: 0,\n        answer: {role: 'assistant', content: 'partial answer', isStreaming: false},\n      });\n    });\n\n    it('commits an open thinking entry too when the provider throws mid-thought', async () => {\n      const h = harness([text('<thinking>half a thou')], {failWith: DROPPED});\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      expect({\n        commits: h.frames().filter(frame => frame.startsWith('commit')),\n        open: stillStreaming(h.session),\n      }).toEqual({\n        commits: ['commit 1'],\n        open: 0,\n      });\n    });\n\n    it('commits the answer, flagged truncated, when a round ends on a tool call with no stop chunk', async () => {\n      const lookup: MessageChunk = {type: 'tool_use', toolCall: {id: 'c1', name: 'lookup', input: {}}};\n      const h = harness([text('Let me check'), lookup]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      const [, answer] = h.session.entries;\n      expect({last: h.frames().slice(-2), open: stillStreaming(h.session), answer}).toMatchObject({\n        last: ['commit 1', 'stream_end'],\n        open: 0,\n        answer: {role: 'assistant', content: 'Let me check', truncated: true},\n      });\n    });\n\n    it('sends one commit per opened entry when the turn ends on a stop chunk', async () => {\n      const h = harness([text('<thinking>pondering</thinking>answer'), STOP]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      expect(h.frames().filter(frame => frame.startsWith('commit'))).toEqual(['commit 1', 'commit 2']);\n    });\n\n    it('records the open entries under suppression without sending a commit when the provider throws', async () => {\n      const h = harness([SUPPRESS, text('hidden')], {failWith: DROPPED});\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      expect({frames: h.frames(), open: stillStreaming(h.session)}).toEqual({\n        frames: ['upsert 0 user \"hello\"', 'skip 1', 'error SERVER_ERROR', 'stream_end'],\n        open: 0,\n      });\n    });\n  });\n\n  describe('an entry cut short', () => {\n    const DROPPED = new Error('provider dropped');\n    const upsertsOf = (sent: OutgoingMessage[], index: number) =>\n      sent.flatMap(frame => (frame.type === 'entry_upsert' && frame.index === index ? [frame.entry] : []));\n\n    it('flags a partial answer truncated in the session and re-sends it with the flag before its commit', async () => {\n      const h = harness([text('partial answer')], {failWith: DROPPED});\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      const [, stored] = h.session.entries;\n      const resent = upsertsOf(h.sent, 1).at(-1);\n      const frames = h.frames();\n      expect({\n        stored,\n        resent,\n        resentBeforeCommit: frames.lastIndexOf('upsert 1 assistant \"partial answer\"') < frames.indexOf('commit 1'),\n      }).toMatchObject({\n        stored: {role: 'assistant', content: 'partial answer', isStreaming: false, truncated: true},\n        resent: {role: 'assistant', content: 'partial answer', truncated: true},\n        resentBeforeCommit: true,\n      });\n    });\n\n    it('flags an open thinking entry truncated when the provider throws mid-thought', async () => {\n      const h = harness([text('<thinking>half a thou')], {failWith: DROPPED});\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      const [, thought] = h.session.entries;\n      expect(thought).toMatchObject({role: 'thinking', isStreaming: false, truncated: true});\n    });\n\n    it('leaves an entry committed on a stop chunk without the flag and sends it once', async () => {\n      const h = harness([text('All done.'), STOP]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      const [, answer] = h.session.entries;\n      expect({flagged: 'truncated' in answer, upserts: upsertsOf(h.sent, 1).length}).toEqual({\n        flagged: false,\n        upserts: 1,\n      });\n    });\n\n    it('flags a suppressed entry that a throw cut short in the session but never re-sends it', async () => {\n      const h = harness([SUPPRESS, text('hidden')], {failWith: DROPPED});\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      const [, hidden] = h.session.entries;\n      expect({hidden, upserts: upsertsOf(h.sent, 1).length}).toMatchObject({\n        hidden: {content: 'hidden', truncated: true},\n        upserts: 0,\n      });\n    });\n\n    it('keeps the text the thinking-tag parser still held when a throw cuts the answer short', async () => {\n      const h = harness([text('Half <thi')], {failWith: DROPPED});\n\n        resent: {content: 'Half <thi', truncated: true},\n      });\n    });\n\n    it('keeps the held text in a suppressed entry too, flagged, without sending any of it', async () => {\n      const h = harness([SUPPRESS, text('Half <thi')], {failWith: DROPPED});\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      const [, hidden] = h.session.entries;\n      expect({hidden, toClient: h.frames().filter(frame => /^\\w+ 1( |$)/.test(frame))}).toMatchObject({\n        hidden: {content: 'Half <thi', truncated: true, isStreaming: false},\n        toClient: ['skip 1'],\n      });\n    });\n  });\n\n  describe('an exhausted tool budget', () => {\n    const SENTENCE = 'A colleague will follow up on this.';\n    const LOOKUP: MessageChunk = {type: 'tool_use', toolCall: {id: 'c1', name: 'lookup', input: {}}};\n    const TOOL_STOP: MessageChunk = {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'tool_use'};\n    const BUDGET_STOP: MessageChunk = {type: 'stop', stopReason: TOOL_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED};\n\n    it(\"renders the hook's sentence as its own committed entry, then stream_end, with no error\", async () => {\n      const h = harness([LOOKUP, TOOL_STOP, text(SENTENCE), BUDGET_STOP]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      const frames = h.frames();\n      expect({tail: frames.slice(-4), errors: frames.filter(frame => frame.startsWith('error'))}).toEqual({\n        tail: ['upsert 2 assistant \"\"', `delta 2 ${JSON.stringify(SENTENCE)}`, 'commit 2', 'stream_end'],\n        errors: [],\n      });\n    });\n\n    it('writes the sentence into the session but only skips it on the wire under suppression', async () => {\n      const h = harness([LOOKUP, TOOL_STOP, SUPPRESS, text(SENTENCE), BUDGET_STOP]);\n\n      await h.send();\n\n      const frames = h.frames();\n      const [, , stored] = h.session.entries;\n      expect({\n        skipped: frames.includes('skip 2'),\n        deltas: frames.filter(frame => frame.startsWith('delta 2')),\n        stored,\n      }).toMatchObject({skipped: true, deltas: [], stored: {role: 'assistant', content: SENTENCE}});\n    });\n\n    it('sends no frame for the synthetic stop when no hook wrote a sentence, so the run ends as it did without one', async () => {\n      const exhausted = harness([LOOKUP, TOOL_STOP, BUDGET_STOP]);\n      const withoutIt = harness([LOOKUP, TOOL_STOP]);\n\n      await exhausted.send();\n      await withoutIt.send();\n\n      const frames = exhausted.frames();\n      expect({frames, last: frames.at(-1)}).toEqual({frames: withoutIt.frames(), last: 'stream_end'});\n    });\n  });\n});\n\nconst userMessage = (content: string) => ({type: 'user_message', content});\nconst afterPendingWork = () => new Promise(resolve => setImmediate(resolve));\n\n// Each run answers with its own number and then waits at its gate, so a test decides when it ends.\nconst gated = (runs: number) => {\n  const sent: OutgoingMessage[] = [];\n  const socket = {\n    readyState: WebSocket.OPEN as number,\n    send: (raw: string) => sent.push(JSON.parse(raw) as OutgoingMessage),\n  };\n  const ws = socket as unknown as WebSocket;\n  const gates = Array.from({length: runs}, () => deferred());\n  const handed: SessionEntry[][] = [];\n  const processMessageStream = async function* (session: ChatSession): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n    const run = handed.length;\n    handed.push(structuredClone(session.entries));\n    yield text(`answer ${run}`);\n    await gates[run].until;\n    yield STOP;\n  };\n  const session: ChatSession = {sessionId: 's1', userId: 'u1', entries: []};\n  const handle = createMessageHandler({processMessageStream} as unknown as ChatOrchestrator);\n\n  return {\n    session,\n    handed,\n    release: (run: number) => gates[run].release(),\n    close: () => {\n      socket.readyState = WebSocket.CLOSED;\n    },\n    frames: () => wire(sent),\n    send: (raw: unknown) => handle(ws, session, raw),\n  };\n};\n\ndescribe('one message at a time per session', () => {\n  it('answers a second user_message only after the first has sent its stream_end, in the order they arrived', async () => {\n    const h = gated(2);\n    const first = h.send(userMessage('A'));\n    const second = h.send(userMessage('B'));\n    await afterPendingWork();\n    h.release(0);\n    await first;\n    h.release(1);\n    await second;\n\n    const {entries} = h.session;\n    expect({\n      entries: entries.map(entry => `${entry.role} ${'content' in entry ? entry.content : ''}`),\n      frames: h.frames(),\n    }).toEqual({\n      entries: ['user A', 'assistant answer 0', 'user B', 'assistant answer 1'],\n      frames: [\n        'upsert 0 user \"A\"',\n        'upsert 1 assistant \"\"',\n        'delta 1 \"answer 0\"',\n        'commit 1',\n        'stream_end',\n        'upsert 2 user \"B\"',\n        'upsert 3 assistant \"\"',\n        'delta 3 \"answer 1\"',\n        'commit 3',\n        'stream_end',\n      ],\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('hands the second run a history whose first answer is finished, never one still streaming', async () => {\n    const h = gated(2);\n    const first = h.send(userMessage('A'));\n    await afterPendingWork();\n    const second = h.send(userMessage('B'));\n    await afterPendingWork();\n    h.release(0);\n    await first;\n    h.release(1);\n    await second;\n\n    expect(h.handed[1]).toEqual([\n      {role: 'user', content: 'A', timestamp: expect.any(String)},\n      {role: 'assistant', content: 'answer 0', timestamp: expect.any(String), isStreaming: false},\n      {role: 'user', content: 'B', timestamp: expect.any(String)},\n    ]);\n  });\n\n  it('answers a ping at once while a run is pending', async () => {\n    const h = gated(1);\n    const run = h.send(userMessage('A'));\n    await afterPendingWork();\n    void h.send({type: 'ping', timestamp: 7});\n    await afterPendingWork();\n    const beforeTheRunEnds = h.frames();\n    h.release(0);\n    await run;\n\n    expect(beforeTheRunEnds).toEqual(['upsert 0 user \"A\"', 'upsert 1 assistant \"\"', 'delta 1 \"answer 0\"', 'pong 7']);\n  });\n\n  it('answers an invalid ping at once but an invalid user_message in turn, after the run in progress', async () => {\n    const h = gated(1);\n    const sends = [h.send(userMessage('A'))];\n    await afterPendingWork();\n    sends.push(h.send({type: 'ping'}), h.send(userMessage('')));\n    await afterPendingWork();\n    const beforeTheRunEnds = h.frames();\n    h.release(0);\n    await \"answer 0\"', 'error INVALID_MESSAGE'],\n      after: ['commit 1', 'stream_end', 'error INVALID_MESSAGE', 'stream_end'],\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('queues three messages sent in one tick before the handler returns, and answers them in that order', async () => {\n    const queued = jest.spyOn(PerSessionLock.prototype, 'run');\n    const h = gated(3);\n    const sends = ['A', 'B', 'C'].map(content => h.send(userMessage(content)));\n    const {calls} = queued.mock;\n    const queuedBeforeAnyAwait = calls.length;\n    for (const run of [0, 1, 2]) h.release(run);\n    await Promise.all(sends);\n    queued.mockRestore();\n\n    const {entries} = h.session;\n    expect({\n      queuedBeforeAnyAwait,\n      asked: entries.filter(entry => entry.role === 'user').map(entry => ('content' in entry ? entry.content : '')),\n    }).toEqual({queuedBeforeAnyAwait: 3, asked: ['A', 'B', 'C']});\n  });\n\n  it('drops a queued message whose socket closed before its turn, without asking the model or recording it', async () => {\n    const h = gated(2);\n    const sends = [h.send(userMessage('A')), h.send(userMessage('B'))];\n    await afterPendingWork();\n    h.close();\n    h.release(0);\n    await Promise.all(sends);\n\n    const {entries} = h.session;\n    expect({\n      asked: h.handed.length,\n      entries: entries.map(entry => `${entry.role} ${'content' in entry ? entry.content : ''}`),\n    }).toEqual({asked: 1, entries: ['user A', 'assistant answer 0']});\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe('a turn whose socket closes', () => {\n  it('abandons the turn mid-stream with no error and no stream_end, committing the cut-off answer as it stood', async () => {\n    const gate = deferred();\n    const h = harness([text('Hal'), text('lo there'), STOP], {pauseAfter: {chunks: 1, until: gate.until}});\n    const connection = new AbortController();\n    const turn = h.send(undefined, connection.signal);\n    await afterPendingWork();\n    const beforeClose = h.frames().length;\n    connection.abort();\n    gate.release();\n    await turn;\n\n    const [, answer] = h.session.entries;\n    expect({afterClose: h.frames().slice(beforeClose), answer}).toEqual({\n      afterClose: ['upsert 1 assistant \"Hal\"', 'commit 1'],\n      answer: {role: 'assistant', content: 'Hal', timestamp: expect.any(String), isStreaming: false, truncated: true},\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('drops a turn whose connection signal is already aborted: records nothing, asks nothing, sends nothing', async () => {\n    const h = harness([text('hi'), STOP]);\n\n    await h.send(undefined, 0,\n      frames: [],\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('hands the run its connection signal', async () => {\n    const handed: Array<AbortSignal | undefined> = [];\n    const orchestrator = {\n      async *processMessageStream(\n        _session: ChatSession,\n        options?: {signal?: AbortSignal}\n      ): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n        handed.push(options?.signal);\n        yield STOP;\n      },\n    } as unknown as ChatOrchestrator;\n    const ws = {send: () => undefined} as unknown as WebSocket;\n    const session: ChatSession = {sessionId: 's1', userId: 'u1', entries: []};\n Promise<string[]> {\n  let requests = 0;\n  const provider: AIProvider = {\n    async *sendMessage(): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n      requests++;\n      const asks: MessageChunk[] = [\n        {type: 'tool_use', toolCall: call},\n        {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'tool_use'},\n      ];\n      yield* requests === 1 ? asks : [text('all done'), STOP];\n    },\n    async generateStructured<T>(): Promise<T> {\n      return {} as T;\n    },\n  };\n  const sent: OutgoingMessage[] = [];\n  const ws = {send: (raw: string) => sent.push(JSON.parse(raw) as OutgoingMessage)} {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01RxhfYcggpv8yKFDs5Qf3Hm","type":"tool_result","content":"6:| Status | In Progress |\n11:Status:         Active\n15:Version 1.0 of the hal-engine WebSocket protocol. It covers, among other things, connection establishment, the message envelope used in both directions, the `SessionEntry` objects a conversation is built from, the entry lifecycle that orders them, the tool execution loop, the heartbeat and termination rules that bound a connection, and the protocol's security considerations. The `Status: Active` line in the block above is the protocol version's own status, distinct from the `Status` row of the header table, which tracks this spec's test-citation coverage under the convention in AGENTS.md § Spec Header Table.\n17:## 1. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01M7vgXsKu9gKxDnz3jDcduc","type":"tool_result","content":"- Any other failure raised while processing a message is reported as `SERVER_ERROR` ([validated by: reports any other failure as a server error](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L320)).\n\n### 5.7 pong\n\nResponse to a client `ping`. The server MUST echo the client's timestamp without modification ([validated by: answers a ping with the timestamp it was given](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L81)).\n\n```json\n{\n  \"type\": \"pong\",\n  \"timestamp\": 1705312200000\n}\n```\n\n| Field       | Type   | Description                             |\n| ----------- | ------ | --------------------------------------- |\n| `type`      | string | `\"pong\"`                                |\n| `timestamp` | number | Echoed from the client's `ping` message |\n\n### 5.8 stream_end\n\nSignals that the server has finished processing a user message. Sent exactly once for every `user_message` the server receives, after all `entry_commit` messages have been sent, whether the run succeeded or failed.\n\n```json\n{\n  \"type\": \"stream_end\"\n}\n```\n\n| Field  | Type   | Description      |\n| ------ | ------ | ---------------- |\n| `type` | string | `\"stream_end\"`   |\n\nSemantics:\n\n- The server MUST send `stream_end` after every `user_message` it receives, including streams where output was suppressed (see [tool-responses.md](../hal-engine-tool-responses/spec.md)), streams that failed after an `error` frame, and a `user_message` that failed validation ([single-terminal-frame](../hal-engine-single-terminal-frame/spec.md)). The one exception is a turn whose socket closes first: it is abandoned, and nothing more is sent ([abandon-on-close](../hal-engine-abandon-on-close/spec.md)).\n- `stream_end` follows every stream even after an error: a failed run sends its `error` frame first and `stream_end` last.\n- An `error` frame does not by itself end a stream. It is advisory: it may concern the run in flight or an unrelated frame, such as a malformed `ping`.\n- The client SHOULD use this message to clear any \"processing\" or \"loading\" indicators.\n- The client MUST treat a run as complete only at `stream_end` or when the socket closes. Unparseable JSON is the one frame answered with `INVALID_FORMAT` and no `stream_end`, because the server cannot tell what it was meant to be; for it the client falls back on the socket closing.\n\n## 6.
